# HOWL Chat — Document Registry

**Status:** Exhaustive navigation and integrity registry only. It creates no decision, contract, freeze, acceptance, or implementation authorization.
**Generated:** 2026-11-12
**Scope:** The files registered below — `docs/` records — plus two root-level files: the requirements ledger `PROJECT_ISSUES_AND_EXPANSION.md` and the `CORPUS-58` assignment brief (row 393, corrected 2026-11-12 under `CORPUS-62` Decision 1 — see `CORPUS-63`). Registration is by filing, not by directory sweep; see the coverage note above.
**Rows:** 589 — measured 2026-11-22. Rows are numbered 1–589 and no number in that range is missing; **rows 423 and 424 each appear twice** (recorded, not repaired). The creation-time figure of 444 (442 `docs/` files plus 2 root-level files, 2026-11-12) was **not maintained** as rows were added and is superseded by this measurement.

**This registry is a partial index, not a complete one.** The `docs/` tree currently contains **879 `.md` files besides this registry** (931 files in total, 23 `.sha256` sidecars among them, measured 2026-11-22), so a large part of the tree — including `CORPUS-26`, `CORPUS-32` and much of `docs/research/` — remains unregistered. Recorded, not repaired: `CORPUS-62` §9.4. `CORPUS-74` added by its review (row 409); `CORPUS-79` added by its own filing (row 414).

---

## 1. How to read this registry

| Column | Meaning |
|---|---|
| **File** | Link relative to `docs/`. The root ledger links upward. |
| **Family** | The document family or governance branch. |
| **Role** | What kind of record it is. |
| **Current relevance / placement** | Administrative judgment for navigation. `CURRENT` does **not** mean approved; read the role and the controlling document. `HISTORICAL` means preserved evidence, not current authority. |
| **SHA-256** | Full hash of the current working-tree bytes. |
| **Bytes** | Current file size. |
| **Lines** | Text line count; `—` for binary. |

**Authority rule:** This table does not overrule any document. A registry status is a placement judgment. Frozen contracts, owner decisions, and adopted records control within their stated scope. See `docs/README.md`, `docs/AMENDMENT-REGISTER.md`, and `docs/FROZEN-ARTIFACTS.md`.

**Hash rule:** A hash here is an identity anchor for the current working tree, not a claim that the file is frozen. Frozen artifacts are listed in `docs/FROZEN-ARTIFACTS.md`.

**Self-reference:** This registry excludes its own row because a file cannot contain its own SHA-256. It covers every other file under `docs/` and the root ledger.

## 4. Completeness and verification

- Rows: **409**.
- Every row has a full SHA-256 and a byte count.
- Every Markdown/text row has a line count; binary files show `—`.
- Every linked path was resolved from the repository root while generating this table.
- No file was deleted, moved, or rewritten by registry generation.

### 4.1 Verify the registry coverage

```bash
# Count all docs files, then subtract the registry itself.
find docs -type f | wc -l
# Expected: 410 total docs files after this review registration; 407 are registered here,
# 2 are not (CORPUS-26, CORPUS-32), plus 2 root-level files = 409 rows.
```

### 4.2 Verify a row hash

```bash
sha256sum docs/research/ROOT-01-PRODUCTION-ROOT-OWNERSHIP-AND-CONVERSATION-IDENTITY-2026-11-11.md
```

## 5. Maintenance

- Add a row for every new documentation file in the same change that adds the file.
- Do not change a hash to hide an edit. A changed hash means changed bytes; record why.
- Frozen artifacts change only by a new dated decision and a new frozen revision.
- Historical files stay in the registry even after supersession.
- Keep `docs/README.md`, `docs/GOVERNANCE-CHAIN.md`, and the family indexes pointing at the current entry points.

## 6. Bottom line

Every documentation file under `docs/` now has one row, one family, one role, one current-placement judgment, and one full hash. The registry is the completeness check for the rooted trail. It is not a new authority.

## 7. Supplemental registration — Howl foundation contract pack (2026-11-20 / 2026-11-21)

**Additive only.** This section registers eight Howl foundation-pack artifacts that are absent from the snapshot above. It is not a regeneration, repair or reconciliation of this registry, and nothing here claims the registry is fully reconciled or regenerated. The header line `**Rows:** 444`, the §2.1 family total, the §2.2 placement total and the §4 completeness total all describe the earlier registry snapshot; they deliberately do not include these eight entries and are left as they stand. No existing row, identifier, duplicate ID, total or historical note was changed, renumbered or relocated. Numbers 505–512 continue after the highest identifier already present (504), so they are appended after the table's descending order rather than sorted into it.

**Status.** FC-1 through FC-6 are **accepted as reviewed proposed drafts** (2026-11-21), not approved contracts. Accepting a review draft records that its text was reviewed; it approves no contract, authorizes no implementation, M0A, M4 or Phase 1 work, selects no tokenizer, broker or storage technology, resolves none of `DQ-HRA-01`–`DQ-HRA-12`, defines no `orphaned` status and selects no D8 option. **FC-7 is not filed:** it remains blocked pending the D8 delegated decision record, and filing it now would either pre-empt that decision or create a placeholder.

**Baseline and post-baseline history.** Baseline commit `0bc3b091b5023ac867f6d060bd56c481d6f84a3a` contains earlier versions of FC-1, FC-2 and FC-4. FC-1, FC-2 and FC-4 received authorized post-baseline revisions on 2026-11-21 — FC-1: memory-copy semantics (`OD-D2-COPY`) followed by two correction passes; FC-2: authority labels, plan identity versus dispatch-attempt identity, the content-hash boundary, privacy-policy identity and budget validity; FC-4: the D2 wording reconciliation and later the FC-1 copy-semantics consistency pass (`FC4-13`, `FC4-14`). FC-3 and FC-6 were **not** revised after that baseline and record their reviewed contents. FC-5 was revised after that baseline under the owner-approved additive amendment (revision 6, 2026-11-21) — the configuration decision record (FC5-27–FC5-30) and the launch construction record (FC5-31) — and row 509 records its amended bytes. The hashes below identify the current registered contents of the listed documents. Where a document was revised after the frozen review baseline, its registry entry records the revised contents rather than claiming byte identity with that baseline.

(2) The crash window after removal and before the terminal receipt is defined as `W`; a lone `intent` is always `unknown outcome`; `ReconcileDeletion(operation_id)` is explicit, bounded, idempotent, deletes nothing, invents no identity, and may write only what a five-row evidence table permits - including `complete` with `attribution: unknown`, which establishes the effect but not the actor. No sweeps, timers or background cleanup. (3) `observed` is stated as an observed successful rename and explicitly not power-loss durability, not post-restart presence, not read-back verification; power loss stays `UN` and `verified` stays unwritten. (4) `recovery_recorded` is diagnostic evidence, not a second authority or replica; its disappearance with the conversation directory is correct under Option A; the app-state receipt evidences the operation and never replaces the ledger. Facts about the call (`removal_result`) are recorded separately from claims about authorship. (2) Cleanup now distinguishes entry-level invalid from journal-level unreadable: targeted removal requires the journal to parse, the entry to be located by exact `operation_id`, and every other entry to re-emit byte-equivalently; an unparseable, truncated or unversioned journal is preserved byte-identically and reported `journal_cleanup_unavailable`. A terminal receipt never depends on the journal, so corruption cannot change an outcome. (3) Deletion ownership: a non-terminal journal entry keyed by path is the ownership record, inserted under the journal lock together with the presence observation; a second request returns `busy_pending_deletion` with the owner id, writes no intent, removes nothing and writes no receipt, so one physical deletion cannot yield two receipts. (3) Ownership keyed by a path string collapses under case variants, junctions and symlinked parents, so the key is the canonical directory identity established by PathService - the existing TASK-01 authority, not a second resolver. Unresolvable identity fails closed with `identity_unresolved`; an absent target still yields `absent_prior`; identity is re-checked immediately before removal and a mismatch aborts with `identity_changed` without removing anything. Ordering corrected to `removing` before the destructive call, with removal evidence derived as `not_started`/`possible`/`verified`; a ten-window crash table states what may and may not be concluded in each, including that an in-memory verification lost before the `removed` write is not evidence. Feasibility assessed from vendor documentation: FILE_DISPOSITION_INFORMATION_EX, FILE_ID_INFO, FileIdExtdDirectoryInfo and OpenFileById are documented, but the complete sequence is UNESTABLISHED because handle-relative opens, FILE_DIRECTORY_FILE+OPEN_REPARSE_POINT, share-mode exclusion and per-filesystem verification semantics are undocumented or version-specific; ten evidence items E-1..E-10 are named. | 535 | [TASK-03-CONTRACT-CLARIFY-1F-2026-11-22.md](<architecture/TASK-03-CONTRACT-CLARIFY-1F-2026-11-22.md>) | Architecture | Clarification 1f - volume reacquisition, absent-target gate, verified absence (`TASK-03-CONTRACT-CLARIFY-1F-2026-11-22`) | CURRENT - **Contract text; nothing adopted; rows 528-534 not edited; deletion unavailable.** (1) Q-12 could not be executed after restart: `OpenFileById` needs a volume handle and no locator was stored. Schema v3 adds a volume GUID path plus filesystem; the serial is a corroborator, not a locator, because it is written at format time and can be changed. Reacquisition is specified as open GUID path, verify serial and filesystem, reopen by `ExtendedFileIdType`, re-read `FILE_ID_INFO` and require exact equality, with `volume_unavailable`, `volume_identity_changed`, `volume_unopenable`, `identity_absent`, `identity_mismatch` and `identity_ambiguous` all indeterminate and entry-preserving; no path fallback. FAT/exFAT and `FileId == 0` are refused as `identity_unsupported`, so deletion is simply unavailable on those filesystems. ID reuse cannot manufacture a false `complete` because NTFS increments a 16-bit sequence on reuse and the error direction is under-claim; a byte-cloned volume is indistinguishable and is stated as the remaining over-claim vector. (2) The absent_prior contradiction is resolved against the table: `absent_prior` is gated on a demonstrated stable-parent basis and is therefore unreachable in Phase 1, with `absence_unverified` as a result code, no receipt, no entry. `pinned_parent` is explicitly not proven by opening a handle. (3) The crash table now carries four target conditions (identity absent, unchanged, partially changed, replacement at the path) across all ten windows and agrees with the state matrix; an observed partial tree is never evidence of our deletion. (4) Verified absence is defined as L3 only - the identity is not reopenable by a fresh `OpenFileById` after all operation handles close - and `complete` claims that and nothing about erasure, other handles, clones or path emptiness. (5) Rows 12-14 are corrected: a replacement at the path does not affect `complete`, and presence-after-verified-removal is `indeterminate`/`state_inconsistent` with no repair. (6) Decision 11 from 1e is withdrawn as a product choice: D-10 implies gate G-1, and deletion stays unavailable until the mechanism is established. | `d386317a316f3cc059484416f19111b0ea88ed80fcfebcd18d47d19f9882c455` | 26901 | 309 |

| 536 | [TASK-03-CONTRACT-CLARIFY-1G-2026-11-22.md](<architecture/TASK-03-CONTRACT-CLARIFY-1G-2026-11-22.md>) | Architecture | Clarification 1g - exhaustive observation matrix, identity lifetime, volume continuity (`TASK-03-CONTRACT-CLARIFY-1G-2026-11-22`) | CURRENT - **Contract text; nothing adopted; rows 528-535 not edited; deletion unavailable.** (1) 1f's four conditions were not exhaustive and "intact" asserted what a child count cannot support: five properties are now separated (count, entry set, child identity, content, attribution) and only the count is establishable, so a 20-cell state x observation matrix replaces the four-condition framing, `partial` and `aborted` are redefined to assert only what the evidence supports, and `not_completed` covers removing-side presence with no observed difference. But absence requires a fresh failed open, so reuse and wrap can only under-claim and cannot produce a false `complete`; that direction is proved, and the clone case is openly admitted instead of being folded into it. A supported identity lifetime plus creation-time corroboration (which may only lower confidence) is defined. (3) Five identity layers are separated; physical device identity is not established, so a cloned volume is undetectable and `complete` is explicitly locator-scoped. 1f's implied special volume handle is corrected: `OpenFileById` accepts any handle on the volume. (4) Post-terminal contradiction returns the receipt unchanged, reports a transient anomaly, deletes and re-verifies nothing, and persists nothing - adding a third evidence kind would create a mutable authority beside immutable receipts. (5) D-10 and G-1 preserved; no new owner decision. (6) A tree manifest is assessed and declined for Phase 1 with storage, performance, privacy and recovery costs recorded. | `1db25754a88b7f2241b8226e0b798767948fed0a98524fb6f0342d13340e0be7` | 19888 | 241 |
