| 547 | [TASK-03-RECONCILIATION-CONTRACT-2026-11-22.md](<architecture/TASK-03-RECONCILIATION-CONTRACT-2026-11-22.md>) | Architecture | TASK-03 replacement contract - deletion and reconciliation (`TASK-03-RECONCILIATION-CONTRACT-2026-11-22`) | CURRENT - **Contract draft; nothing adopted; no implementation authorized; rows 528-546 unedited and now historical.** Replaces the purpose of the identity-proof series with a question that is answerable without privilege: not "was this exact identity destroyed" but "is this conversation data still available". Automated deletion, a JSON worklist entry as a hint, and a read-only scanner that reconciles the index. Grounded in the real storage contract (source-derived from persistence.go): meta.json always, history.ndjson the source of truth with legacy history.json, conditionally written world/scenario/characters/lorebooks/memories files. Manifests record required paths, alternatives groups and optional files, generated at creation from the storage contract version and **never** from disk, so a damaged conversation cannot redefine its own requirements and appear healthy. Six derived states - Healthy, Pending reconciliation, Missing, Damaged, Unavailable, Orphaned - with six forbidden transitions; a scan error is never Missing and Unavailable never becomes Healthy. Damaged is the state a failed deletion produces and is visible rather than silently kept or silently removed. The worklist is an optimization whose ordering is a speed decision only, because the full sweep is authority; orphans are reported and never imported or deleted; the scanner never writes to conversation data. Cadence is startup plus periodic with jitter, resume-from-sleep and manual refresh, bounded per pass, with a persistent attempt counter and per-record backoff. Deletion scope is confined to the conversation subtree - the only identity rule retained, constraining what may be deleted rather than what may be claimed. No receipt, no authorship claim, no elevation, no USN. 16 acceptance tests and five open questions (UNC roots, case sensitivity, meta-only conversations, repair path, manifest versioning). | `79d9de841191a5a6bbd1e659a8b622bcdf113958f4f31f7c36998dee544f1f21` | 13890 | 205 |

| 548 | [TASK-03-RECONCILIATION-OQ3-2026-11-22.md](<architecture/TASK-03-RECONCILIATION-OQ3-2026-11-22.md>) | Architecture | TASK-03 OQ-3 resolved - empty conversations, and a stale-history finding (`TASK-03-RECONCILIATION-OQ3-2026-11-22`) | CURRENT - **Contract addendum; amends row 547 by addition; row 547 unedited; no implementation authorized.** OQ-3 verified against the writer lifecycle by observation on a scratch root: after NewChat the directory holds meta.json, history.json = "[]" (2 bytes), characters/lorebooks/memories, and NO history.ndjson; history.ndjson appears only after the first AppendHistoryEntry, and world.json/scenario.json are absent while nil. Decision recorded: empty conversations are Healthy, and history becomes required only when content is first committed, with existence, content and expectation kept as three separate distinctions. **Second finding, unexpected:** history.json is a lagging snapshot - it stayed "[]" after the first committed message - so row 547 RC-09's "at least one of ndjson or history.json" was wrong in both directions: it would call an empty conversation Damaged, and would call a conversation whose ndjson was deleted Healthy because a 2-byte placeholder still existed. The accepted representation is now version-scoped (legacy populated history.json; current history.ndjson) and **non-empty** is a property of content, not existence. Expectation flips AFTER the commit, so a crash between leaves Healthy rather than a false Damaged; UpdatedAt is explicitly not the expectation because SaveChat rewrites it for empty conversations too. meta.json absent in a present directory is Damaged, not Missing, because the data may still be there. Eight tests added; OQ-3a/3b/3c left open (non-append writers, the exact empty size, dual-state legacy conversations). | `a40e5bfafd040987edcda1631755fb2157c879952481ffca8ae396d2660edf39` | 7197 | 130 |

| 549 | [TASK-03-RECONCILIATION-OQ3A-2026-11-22.md](<architecture/TASK-03-RECONCILIATION-OQ3A-2026-11-22.md>) | Architecture | TASK-03 OQ-3a writer-lifecycle inventory (`TASK-03-RECONCILIATION-OQ3A-2026-11-22`) | CURRENT - **Read-only investigation; contract addendum; supersedes row 548 RC2-05; no code changed; no implementation authorized.** Ten content-creation paths inventoried with writer, representation, commit boundary, expectation and crash window. **SaveChatAs is the finding:** observed on a scratch root, a duplicate carries content in history.json (503 b) and has NO history.ndjson, while LoadChat returns its 3 messages - so row 548's version-scoped representation rule would have called every duplicated conversation Damaged. Acceptance is now content-based (ndjson with a record, or history.json larger than the 2-byte "[]" form), which also settles the stale-snapshot worry correctly: ndjson deleted but populated history.json present is Healthy because the content is still loadable, and damage means content unavailable rather than a particular file missing. **Creation can collide:** generateChatID has one-second resolution and NewChat uses MkdirAll without exclusivity; two chats in one second observed sharing an ID, and the first probe run showed SaveChatAs reusing the source directory, overwriting its meta.json and leaving the source ndjson in place. **Commit target is a global pointer** - SaveChat sets LastChatID and every write path resolves through GetLastChatID - so after SaveChatAs messages and final save target the copy (source-derived). **Observation gap handled, not hand-waved:** the marker is explicitly not claimed crash-safe; a three-valued marker (none/pending/committed) written BEFORE the append makes the unknowable case visible as Pending reconciliation instead of silently Healthy, is monotonic, and can never produce a false Damaged; the residual window is stated as a limit. Existing tests cover SaveChatAs content but cannot see the representation gap; ten RT3 tests added. Unestablished: collision frequency, SaveChatAs pointer intent, future import reuse, exact empty threshold. | `c2be874e51de89b57c71f9bc43892c9e84f24d356c9f77f0f0ce88c501e7a18d` | 10720 | 157 |

| 550 | [TASK-03-CREATION-IDENTITY-DEFECT-2026-11-22.md](<architecture/TASK-03-CREATION-IDENTITY-DEFECT-2026-11-22.md>) | Architecture | TASK-03 creation identity defect, recorded separately (`TASK-03-CREATION-IDENTITY-DEFECT-2026-11-22`) | CURRENT - **Defect record, kept separate from reconciliation; no repair authorized; no code changed.** Promoted to its own row on the owner's instruction rather than folded into the manifest work. Mechanism: generateChatID has one-second resolution, NewChat uses MkdirAll without exclusivity, SaveChat overwrites meta.json and history.json, and SaveChat repoints the global LastChatID - so two creations in one second share a directory and the later one silently adopts the earlier identity; observed consequence was SaveChatAs reusing the source directory and leaving its ndjson under new metadata. Intended invariant recorded as four normative clauses: unique ID, exclusive directory creation, never adopt an existing directory, fail loudly without touching the existing conversation. Reconciliation owes it only detection and reporting - never repair, never rename, never split, and no remedy that deletes either party's data - with the open point that content alone may not distinguish one damaged conversation from two sharing a directory. Suggested as a separate foundational task (TASK-04, not started). Unestablished: real-world frequency, window/retry exposure, whether existing conversations were already created this way. | `9d1b51ff0c46f734b87efad40467fd09870813299c79b9ee293ba08a85103b48` | 4596 | 82 |
| 551 | [TASK-03-RECONCILIATION-OQ3B-2026-11-22.md](<architecture/TASK-03-RECONCILIATION-OQ3B-2026-11-22.md>) | Architecture | TASK-03 OQ-3b settled: content-based representation, empty vs malformed, scan cost (`TASK-03-RECONCILIATION-OQ3B-2026-11-22`) | CURRENT - **Contract addendum; read-only investigation; no code changed; no implementation authorized.** States the representation rule once, as the question "can Howl still load the committed history": history.json = [] is not content, a populated history.json is valid without ndjson, a populated ndjson is valid without the snapshot, missing one is not damage if another preserves the content, and none-with-expectation is Damaged - with row 548's version-scoped rule kept explicitly superseded rather than quietly dropped. **Empty forms measured:** history.json [] = 2 bytes, RewriteHistory([]) = 0 bytes, one stray newline = 1 byte and is neither. **Malformed is indistinguishable from empty:** truncated json, a non-array object and an array of strings all load as 0 messages with nil error, and torn or corrupt ndjson loads as a silent shorter prefix (3 records cut mid-record loaded as 2; 2 good + corrupt + 2 good loaded as 2), so the loader is lossy by design and cannot be the scanner's observation primitive - Healthy means the loadable prefix is present, not that every committed record is. Promotion is therefore grounded in a successful parse yielding at least one record; malformed never promotes. **Cost measured** (best of five, this machine): 100 messages 0.50 ms, 1,000 1.54 ms, 10,000 10.2 ms, 100,000 104.7 ms, against stat 36-39 us and a final-byte read 2-3 us - so availability needs no parse, counting does, and the scan is tiered. LoadChat leaves the directory byte-identical (hash unchanged over three loads): safe to call, still not authoritative. Marker must persist through DurableStore with its own crash test (testHookBeforeReplace exists), else the ambiguity just moves to another file. Nine tests added. Open: exact threshold constants, whether a missing final newline is Damaged or a warning, and marker document placement. | `2daf6165a652d681d1b196354cd998fda2b9d6d0437858f074aea8758ab7bec4` | 8169 | 148 |

| 552 | [TASK-03-RECONCILIATION-OQ3C-2026-11-22.md](<architecture/TASK-03-RECONCILIATION-OQ3C-2026-11-22.md>) | Architecture | TASK-03 OQ-3c settled: coexistence, strict validity, promotion tightened (`TASK-03-RECONCILIATION-OQ3C-2026-11-22`) | CURRENT - **Contract addendum; read-only investigation; no code changed; no implementation authorized.** Records the four-property distinction (loadability vs structural validity vs content presence vs completeness), with LoadChat explicitly demoted: it is a safe read-only observation tool and never a validity test, because it can prefer the lossier representation and return a clean-looking count while records still on disk are ignored. **Coexistence is not benign - measured on ten states.** Distinct content is possible and ndjson takes precedence (json 1 record vs ndjson 1 record of different text -> the ndjson text); with ndjson 1 record and json 3 the loader returned 1, hiding two records that exist; with a torn log and a valid fuller snapshot the loader returned the torn prefix; and a zero-byte log beside a populated snapshot **resurrected deleted content** (returned 2, "deleted-a"), which is the RewriteHistory two-write window: log renamed first, snapshot written second. Consequences recorded: precedence is a display rule and not an evidence rule; freshness cannot be inferred from size or timestamp; and a zero-byte-log-with-snapshot state is Inconsistent, never content-present. **Promotion tightened:** row 551 RC4-12's "parses and yields at least one record" was satisfiable by a truncated log with two good records and a corrupt tail, so structural validity is now whole-representation (every line parses plus a final newline; whole json document parses as an array), any invalid representation blocks promotion even if another is valid, disagreement uses the larger valid set as the completeness basis and is reported, and a torn tail is real loss (Damaged, reason truncated). Reason vocabulary empty/malformed/truncated/inconsistent/absent added without changing the six states. Validation is an on-decision cost, not a per-pass one; the performance numbers remain evidence, not a budget. Nine tests added. Open: whether the app should stop preferring a torn log, whether the RewriteHistory window deserves its own defect record, and whether inconsistent is ever user-actionable. | `909ac09d5713f48c159b3b38180a70db59d6d23d81270c05da51a5fec2c90087` | 8872 | 141 |

| 553 | [TASK-03-CONTRACT-AUDIT-2026-11-22.md](<architecture/TASK-03-CONTRACT-AUDIT-2026-11-22.md>) | Architecture | TASK-03 audit of the replacement contract against all reconciliation findings (`TASK-03-CONTRACT-AUDIT-2026-11-22`) | CURRENT - **Audit; nothing adopted; row 547 not edited; no implementation authorized.** Opens by superseding row 552 RC6-11 on the owner's objection: count is not provenance, disagreement fails closed as Inconsistent with no winner, provenance is testable only as an exact-prefix relation, and record IDs cannot carry that test because they are positional indices (AppendChatMessage writes len-1, ReplaceChatHistory writes i, so a delete renumbers the tail). A prefix relation is diagnostic only - it bounds the possibilities to stale-short vs resurrected-long and cannot resolve them. **Audited against six questions** (presence, structural validity, content presence, completeness, coexistence, promotion): row 547 answered one inadequately and left four unaddressed. **Contradictions found:** RC-05 mislabels history.json as legacy when SaveChat, RewriteHistory and SaveChatAs all write it; RC-09 requires a history file even for an empty conversation; RC-11 defines Healthy by path existence, which a 2-byte "[]" satisfies; RC-11 Missing contradicts RC-12 by absorbing present meta.json; RC-14 forbids the marker write; RC-15 has no row for truncated, inconsistent or malformed; and **RT-05 is now wrong** - ndjson removed with a populated history.json is Healthy, not Damaged, because the content still loads. **New finding RC7-09:** a clear is a retraction and the marker needs a fourth value, since RewriteHistory([]) leaves no trace and committed-plus-empty would otherwise mark every deliberately cleared conversation Damaged forever. Inconsistent maps to Unavailable rather than a seventh state. Defect boundaries stated inside the audit: creation collision (row 550), loader prefers a torn log (OQ-3c-a) and RewriteHistory resurrection (OQ-3c-b) stay separate, and the scanner may not compensate for any of them. v2 is specified as a consolidation rather than another addendum. | `965eb92cc6189798580e03746406c923ef4c5ac432602d2c7f7bc4ea8e526c21` | 13313 | 189 |

| 554 | [TASK-03-RECONCILIATION-CONTRACT-V2-2026-11-22.md](<architecture/TASK-03-RECONCILIATION-CONTRACT-V2-2026-11-22.md>) | Architecture | TASK-03 reconciliation contract v2, consolidated (`TASK-03-RECONCILIATION-CONTRACT-V2-2026-11-22`) | CURRENT - **Contract v2 draft; supersedes row 547, which is retained unedited; nothing adopted; no implementation authorized.** A consolidation, not another addendum: 50 clauses and 20 tests that replace their predecessors rather than stack on them. Carries forward the four-property basis with every state-determining clause naming which property it rests on; LoadChat demoted to a loadability mechanism; count-based selection withdrawn; disagreement fails closed as Inconsistent mapping to Unavailable; prefix evidence interpreted by direction against the writer lifecycle (snapshot prefix of log is expected lag, log prefix of snapshot is not) but never proof of provenance or completeness; record IDs explicitly unable to establish it because they are positional indices. **Generation added on the owner's objection:** four scalar marker values are not sufficient, so the expectation record carries state+generation, a clear closes generation G and the next commit begins G+1, and the generation bounds attribution between a retracted snapshot and new content - while stating plainly that it cannot identify which records belong to which lineage, which remains unestablished. Marker written BEFORE the content change (superseding the 549 ordering), persisted through DurableStore with a crash test at testHookBeforeReplace. **Six transitions with crash points and the scanner's required report:** creation, first append, subsequent append, correction/retraction, clear, append-after-clear - including a clear that recorded intent but did not finish reported as Pending, never Damaged and never Healthy-with-content. cleared is explicitly denied cleanup authority: it records intent and changes interpretation only, and no marker value authorises deleting residue. Defect boundaries restated in the contract itself (row 550, OQ-3c-a, OQ-3c-b) with a clause forbidding the scanner from compensating for any of them. | `d38124d29335d31ea7a866bb9537425ef51cfb58406bb17e4081cb360d26e189` | 20307 | 336 |

| 555 | [TASK-03-RECONCILIATION-GATE-2026-11-22.md](<architecture/TASK-03-RECONCILIATION-GATE-2026-11-22.md>) | Architecture | TASK-03 v2 amendment: unresolved-transition mutation gate (`TASK-03-RECONCILIATION-GATE-2026-11-22`) | CURRENT - **Amendment to row 554, adding V2-51…V2-66; contract work only; no implementation authorized.** Challenges the six v2 transitions against one invariant and finds it **not enforced**: with gen G committed and a clear interrupted after its intent was recorded, an ordinary append succeeded (error nil) and the log ended up holding three G records plus one G+1 record, which LoadChat returned as four messages; the marker file was never read. Corroborated in source - AppendHistoryEntry opens with O_APPEND|O_CREATE and checks no state, the chat package has no pending/unresolved/recovery logic at all, and App.startup does nothing about chat persistence. **Consequence:** a mixed-generation log is byte-indistinguishable from a single-generation conversation, so this can never be caught after the fact - prevention, not detection - which upgrades V2-OQ-1 from an attribution refinement to a correctness question. **The gate:** while a transition is unresolved no mutation may append or rewrite; the writer completes the operation or refuses with a typed failure (never queues, since the base generation is unknown); "resolved" is defined per operation (clear is resolved only when the marker is cleared AND both representations are structurally valid AND empty); G+1 may accept writes only after G closes; the application finishes or refuses while the scanner does neither. Promotion now requires generation-safe provenance, the snapshot-prefix-of-log rule is qualified by log validity and transition settlement, and the DurableStore test must cover (state, generation) pair consistency rather than a single value. All six audit areas answered with verdicts; eight tests added, one of them asserting the contract's limit rather than passing silently. Unestablished: whether the five enumerated mutation paths are a closed inventory, since a sixth path added later reopens the hole. | `c0d1e3d971e2f75c79e793dc90748bcdc5f67087bbbd2c841632460143188ffa` | 8607 | 110 |

| 556 | [TASK-03-GATE-CALLGRAPH-2026-11-22.md](<architecture/TASK-03-GATE-CALLGRAPH-2026-11-22.md>) | Architecture | TASK-03 gate audit against the mutation call graph (`TASK-03-GATE-CALLGRAPH-2026-11-22`) | CURRENT - **Amendment to row 555, adding V2-67…V2-82; source reads only; no implementation authorized.** Audits the gate against the real call graph and finds row 555 unenforceable as written: PersistenceManager has no mutex, no lock file and no atomic check-and-write, and Service.mu covers only in-memory service state, so a caller-side check races by construction. **The inventory was an undercount:** four writers reached from at least twelve entry points (SaveChat from NewChat, SaveChatAs, RenameChat, performSave, SaveChatData and character edits; AppendHistoryEntry from user, assistant and audio turns; RewriteHistory from edit, clear and final save; RemoveAll from delete). **Three writers sit outside PersistenceManager entirely** - memory service writes memories.json and memories_backup.json, auto-capture settings write auto_capture_config.json, continuity writes continuity_config.json - and SaveChat also writes memories.json, so that file has two uncoordinated writers; those are declared out of scope rather than absorbed. **Two bypasses found:** App.LoadChat saves the previous conversation before switching, so a read path mutates, and WipeAllData renames the whole chats root, which no per-conversation gate can cover. Row 547 RC-27 (instances need no coordination) is superseded - with a marker and a gate, two instances can interleave a check and a write. Gate now requires check-and-write in one critical section, a single enforcement point, and cross-process serialization. The three preserved requirements are restated: idempotent clear retry, cleared plus both representations valid and empty, and an inventory maintenance rule where a new mutation path must add a gate-coverage test. Eight tests added, one a deliberate meta-test. Unestablished: whether Chats and chats are the same directory on a case-sensitive volume, and whether memories_backup.json being overwritten with {} before every save is intentional. | `39fc707703ae42bb50fbec233b0e650e73c3b0d07edf1c5c5c53ba2e411eff97` | 8613 | 110 |

| 557 | [TASK-03-DURABLE-PAIR-2026-11-22.md](<architecture/TASK-03-DURABLE-PAIR-2026-11-22.md>) | Architecture | TASK-03 DurableStore pair consistency closed (`TASK-03-DURABLE-PAIR-2026-11-22`) | CURRENT - **Adds V2-83…V2-89; contract work only; no test written or executed.** Closes the last open item in row 556's assessment table. **Demonstrated:** TestCrashBeforeReplaceLeavesPreviousDocument kills a real child at testHookBeforeReplace and asserts the previous document is intact and Load reports Recovered(interrupted_commit_rolled_back); the live path's only mutation is a rename, so no reader sees a partial file. Both crash windows are safe - before the rename the previous document survives with a rollback reason, after the rename the new document matches its manifest and staged leftovers are swept. **Therefore a mixed (state, generation) pair is impossible by construction iff the marker is one document** - that is now an explicit schema constraint, not a test hope. **Two derived requirements:** a marker read returning Recovered is internally consistent but possibly stale, since Result.Current() is false for it, so it never counts as evidence that a transition is settled and the gate refuses while reconciliation reports Pending; and the boundary needs **compare-and-swap on the marker under one lock hold**, because Load is lock-free and Save takes the lock internally, so read-then-save is exactly the caller-side check V2-74 forbids. Save as it exists cannot express conditional replace - a design requirement on the eventual implementation, not a defect in the store. Three tests added; VT-39 is the marker-level counterpart of an existing store test and is not a new proof of rename atomicity. Store passing status is Source-derived from its test source, not re-observed this session. | `fd725e35599fb192ca752b16992286207f1dc3b5ba3892b0b658a512b4806914` | 5938 | 76 |

| 558 | [TASK-03-FINAL-AUDIT-2026-11-22.md](<architecture/TASK-03-FINAL-AUDIT-2026-11-22.md>) | Architecture | TASK-03 v2 final audit: the addendum stack contradicts the decision table (`TASK-03-FINAL-AUDIT-2026-11-22`) | CURRENT - **Adds V2-90…V2-104 and VT-40…VT-47; contract work only; nothing authorized.** Audits v2 plus rows 555-557 against each other and finds **eleven contradictions with one cause**: the decision table is the only executable surface ("applied in order, first match wins") and every amendment imposed preconditions on rows 5/6/8 from outside it, so a scanner built from the table alone reproduces all of them - v2 cannot be closed by addendum and needs a v3 consolidation with conditions inline. **Findings:** content with marker `none` matches no row, and VT-18 asserts it is Healthy, which would be exactly the probe-12 mixture legitimised (fail-closed resolution: Pending, `unattributed`, and duplication writes its own marker); a 0-byte log is simultaneously invalid under V2-09 and empty under V2-10, and that file is the normal clear outcome; row 8 can return Healthy on a `Recovered` marker that V2-86 says is not authoritative; row 6 calls unattributable content `retracted-residue`, asserting the provenance V2-16 denies; V2-19 says no state is stored while the marker stores one; V2-55 forbids the scanner to resolve transitions while requiring it to promote; deletion is exempt under V2-53 and gated under V2-75; v2 is silent on concurrent instances after superseding RC-27; the marker ordering table has no row for duplication or for content appearing without an append; V2-13 overstates the store (a first marker write has no previous document, so the outcome is absent, not previous-or-new); and row 5 versus V2-32 disagree about creation. Proposes V2-91…V2-103 one by one, replaces VT-18, and retains CAS as an unimplemented design requirement. Evidence classification corrected: pair safety remains Source-derived and holds for documents that already existed. | `166dac474051ace1038f165419f0095a2164b98bc4d492db6aeeb9ccab60f8ae` | 11615 | 123 |

| 559 | [TASK-03-RECONCILIATION-CONTRACT-V3-2026-11-22.md](<architecture/TASK-03-RECONCILIATION-CONTRACT-V3-2026-11-22.md>) | Architecture | TASK-03 reconciliation contract v3, self-contained consolidation (`TASK-03-RECONCILIATION-CONTRACT-V3-2026-11-22`) | CURRENT - **Draft contract, 61 clauses, 20 decision rows, 8 lifecycle tables, 41 tests; nothing adopted; no implementation authorized.** Built on the governing principle that there is one authoritative ordered decision procedure and no state may depend on a condition hidden in an amendment, footnote, narrative or test expectation. **Self-contained:** it does not require rows 547-558 to be read to be executed; older texts remain evidence and are never normative. **Resolves the eleven contradictions of row 558 by folding conditions into the table:** zero-byte NDJSON is now vacuously valid, so a completed clear has a defined interpretation; `none`/absent marker with content is Pending `unattributed`, never Healthy, and duplication writes its own `(pending,1)`/`(committed,1)`, so a conforming duplicate never reaches that row; a `Recovered` marker reaches row 5 and stops, never settling or promoting; `retracted-residue` is retained as vocabulary only because lineage cannot be established and is never emitted; row 8 distinguishes `committed` with void content (Damaged) from `none` with no content (Healthy empty); marker resolution is separated from content settlement; deletion is exempt from the gate by statement and is not a resolution; first marker write is distinguished from replacement, where a death yields `Absent` with no pre-image. **The keystone:** `gate_enforced` is a declared writer property consulted by rows 18-19, so content in generation 2+ is Pending `promotion_requires_gate` while the gate does not exist rather than Healthy on an assumption - when it is implemented, rows 16-17 become reachable with no other change. **Mechanical check:** every state-producing test names its row, and an assertion requires every row to be covered, so a rule change cannot leave a test preserving an obsolete expectation. Restraint preserved throughout: gate, CAS and cross-process serialization specified but unimplemented; per-representation stamps Unestablished; rows 550, OQ-3c-a and OQ-3c-b separately owned. | `475ca21dfe0639b707ce130777f8ecee1bc81d527a6f50930fad74668cd16eac` | 26697 | 385 |

| 560 | [TASK-03-RECONCILIATION-CONTRACT-V3-1-2026-11-22.md](<architecture/TASK-03-RECONCILIATION-CONTRACT-V3-1-2026-11-22.md>) | Architecture | TASK-03 reconciliation contract v3.1, self-contained (`TASK-03-RECONCILIATION-CONTRACT-V3-1-2026-11-22`) | CURRENT - **Supersedes v3 (row 559) wholly; 75 clauses, 20 decision rows, 8 lifecycle tables, 47 tests; nothing adopted; no implementation authorized.** Resolves the two open V3 questions as a full consolidation rather than an amendment, because amending V3 would recreate the addendum-stack defect V3 removed. **Two authorities separated:** availability is decided by §4 from observation, mutation permission by §3, and a state never grants permission - Healthy does not mean writable, Pending does not mean blocked. **Generation >=2 fail-closed retained, on an availability finding rather than a bookkeeping one:** a legitimate G+1 history (clear settled, then append) and a contaminated one (clear interrupted before the rewrite, then append into a log still holding G) are byte-identical in structure, size, ordering and content, and the marker has no history, so availability itself is unestablished; classifying such a conversation Healthy would present retracted content as confirmed, which is the resurrection harm reached by reading. Reason renamed `attribution_unestablished`. **gate_enforced provenance fixed:** derived from a build-bound enforcement self-test, default false, false on any doubt, never a marker field, config flag, setting or asserted boolean, and never inferred from the absence of contamination. **Enforcement does not certify the past**, so shipping the gate will not retroactively clear existing G>=2 conversations - opened as OQ-7, with OQ-6 for recording clear settlement (which is why "generation 2 with a known completed clear" is not currently a describable input). **Five review cases answered:** G1 stays Healthy; G>=2 pending because availability is unestablished; the known-clear case is unobservable; post-append content is displayable but not Healthy; a recovered marker is row 5 first. Final decision-table review: totality, mutual exclusivity, no external dependency, and three stated asymmetries. | `b7421bd051b3cbf27c3f1b14d5fd0149256b29c36375ddc3410e76eac7d62868` | 30124 | 405 |

| 561 | [TASK-03-BASELINE-ACCEPTANCE-2026-11-22.md](<architecture/TASK-03-BASELINE-ACCEPTANCE-2026-11-22.md>) | Architecture | TASK-03: V3.1 accepted as the contract baseline (`TASK-03-BASELINE-ACCEPTANCE-2026-11-22`) | CURRENT - **Acceptance record; changes no clause of row 560; no implementation authorized.** V3.1 is the single authoritative ordered decision procedure for TASK-03, with OQ-6 (settlement evidence) and OQ-7 (enforcement evidence) explicitly open rather than treated as reasons to reopen the table. Acceptance is of the contract, not of any mechanism in it: gate, CAS, cross-process serialization, `gate_enforced`, settlement record and enforcement record are all specified and none implemented, so no code, migration, marker creation, gate activation, deletion enablement, duplication change or persistence change follows. States the two-record rule formally - a settlement record cannot certify enforcement and an enforcement record cannot certify that earlier generations were settled, neither is derivable from the other, and neither may be inferred from the absence of observed contamination - and requires each to carry its own crash-consistency and lifecycle definition so the ambiguity is not merely moved into the new evidence mechanism. | `c078b00c91a3e715a401e36be121dc69ec17d0c2fb2a9a54b12193fd038672ee` | 2270 | 38 |
| 562 | [TASK-03-OQ6-SETTLEMENT-2026-11-22.md](<architecture/TASK-03-OQ6-SETTLEMENT-2026-11-22.md>) | Architecture | TASK-03 OQ-6: settlement evidence design investigation (`TASK-03-OQ6-SETTLEMENT-2026-11-22`) | CURRENT - **Proposal only; 21 clauses, 6 tests; V3.1 unchanged; no implementation authorized.** Restates the question as being about the past rather than the present: V3-31 defines settlement by observation and it is checkable while true, but once the marker advances the observation is gone. A settlement record must therefore prove completion at a point the append could not precede, and the second half of that is a property of the writer, not of the record - so settlement evidence alone can never establish purity. **Recommends extending the existing marker with `settled_generation` and `settled_at` rather than creating a second document:** one document means the same rename replaces pair and settlement, inheriting atomicity, CAS, pre-images and crash tests with no new crash domain, whereas a separate file would add a lifecycle and a two-document consistency problem. The field is load-bearing only if the next generation's CAS compares `(state, generation, settled_generation)`; stored but uncompared it is a comment. **Staleness fails closed:** a `Recovered` marker carries an older settlement, so it under-certifies and no recovery direction over-certifies - the property that makes the single-document option safe. **No backfill:** settlement must never be inferred by scanner, migration or repair, so historical generation >=2 conversations stay Pending even after the field ships. Purity is the conjunction of settlement and enforcement evidence, never either alone. Table effect limited to rows 16-17 gaining `settled_generation >= G-1`; their coverage stays declared-inactive until both capabilities exist. Recommends not pursuing it before OQ-7, since a settlement record without an enforced boundary proves only what a writer asserted while appearing to prove more. OQ-5 schema versioning flagged, not decided. | `7ba815b5271644681110fdf32d5b40a8783d9d987e8599feaadc6d029dec014e` | 8305 | 127 |

| 563 | [TASK-03-OQ7-ENFORCEMENT-2026-11-22.md](<architecture/TASK-03-OQ7-ENFORCEMENT-2026-11-22.md>) | Architecture | TASK-03 OQ-7: enforcement evidence, read-only investigation (`TASK-03-OQ7-ENFORCEMENT-2026-11-22`) | CURRENT - **Read-only design investigation; 25 clauses, 8 tests; V3.1 unchanged; no implementation authorized.** Central issue stated first: **atomic is not truthful** - DurableStore guarantees the marker is whole, never that it is true; truth needs the writer to have held exclusivity across the transition and no path to bypass the boundary. **Q1 authority:** the premise is currently false (four writers, twelve entry points, three external writers, row 556), so no certificate can exist; of the three candidate proofs only the unforgeable capability type checked by the compiler is authority, with a static build assertion as a second net - a startup self-test is regression evidence only, because its coverage is exactly the paths someone remembered. **Q2 exclusivity:** the primitive is adequate and Source-derived - LockFileEx exclusive/fail-immediately on Windows, flock LOCK_EX|LOCK_NB elsewhere, cross-process and fail-fast - but the composition is absent since no history write takes a lock and the marker CAS does not exist; the lock is advisory, which is why the capability type rather than the lock is what makes the property provable. **Q3 lifecycle:** an OS lock cannot be withdrawn while held and is released on process death, so there is no stale-lock problem; but enforcement can be bypassed entirely while running, and unavailability currently becomes **silent non-persistence** because at least four call sites discard the error. **Q4 crash:** all seven death points across the transition are fail-closed and none reads as Healthy-but-wrong; point 3 is the important one - both representations void and valid yet settlement unrecorded - which is exactly why OQ-6's S-06 is superseded: the precondition for settlement is the writer's continuous hold, not the observation of emptiness. `settled_at` demoted to audit metadata that no row may read. **Verdict:** conditional yes under five conditions; absent the first two the field is an assertion by an unverified writer, atomic and recoverable but evidentially worthless, and without the fifth it is worse still, because the failure is silent. | `371a27023eb3174332785823de63d6ee49fd881032810698622cbb552e371e71` | 11423 | 144 |

| 564 | [TASK-03-OQ7-LIFETIME-CONTAINMENT-2026-11-22.md](<architecture/TASK-03-OQ7-LIFETIME-CONTAINMENT-2026-11-22.md>) | Architecture | TASK-03 OQ-7 clarification 1: capability lifetime and write containment (`TASK-03-OQ7-LIFETIME-CONTAINMENT-2026-11-22`) | CURRENT - **Contract-level clarification; 19 clauses; V3.1 unchanged; no implementation authorized.** Corrects the phrase "unforgeable capability", which overstated what Go can express: a token can be stored, copied, retained or reused after release, and no type-level construction proves that the calling code currently holds a lock - there is no goroutine-local state to check against and the compiler, vet and race detector say nothing about lock ownership across a call. The claim is split into four decidable properties (non-constructible, non-retainable, non-reusable, containment) and the verdict is that an API shape satisfies all four while a token struggles with three. **Recommended shape:** keep the raw writer unexported and expose one `boundary.Mutate(chatID, command)` that acquires the lock, validates the marker, evaluates the command's precondition, refuses when unresolved, mutates content, records the marker CAS and releases - so no capability value exists to outlive the call. The command set is closed and typed with no function-valued fields: a callback would run caller code inside the hold where it could block, re-enter, panic or retain what it was given, and a caller-supplied "expected pair" would move the read-then-write race of V3-28 up one layer. Five requirements made decidable, plus the rule that partial success is never reported as success - a table of five content/marker orderings, each mapped to the fail-closed row the caller must see. **On the build assertion:** it must be negative (the set of sites opening a history representation for writing is a subset of the boundary package), not a checklist of known writers; its completeness depends on conversation paths being centralised through one constructor, and where that does not hold it is a heuristic that must be described as one - Unestablished today. Runs as a build gate, never a disableable lint. O7-22 restated accordingly; OQ-6 gains no trust from this, only a more precisely bounded deficit. Four sub-questions opened: path centralisation, lock scope, re-entrancy, and where the non-history writers belong. | `0328e436a57c9a93e87d03d497296a8bb9188a8ebcf8ea3801b5fd8c67fdf412` | 8506 | 117 |

| 565 | [TASK-03-OQ7A-PATH-CENTRALISATION-2026-11-22.md](<architecture/TASK-03-OQ7A-PATH-CENTRALISATION-2026-11-22.md>) | Architecture | TASK-03 OQ-7a: path centralisation, read-only inventory (`TASK-03-OQ7A-PATH-CENTRALISATION-2026-11-22`) | CURRENT - **Read-only investigation; 19 clauses, 6 tests; V3.1 unchanged; no implementation authorized.** Answers the central dependency of the OQ-7 branch. **Correction first:** a failure response is not a rollback - refusal before any mutation leaves no consistency debt, while content mutated with the marker unrecorded is an unresolved transition on disk that reconciliation must see as unresolved (V3 rows 12/18/19, or 7 when torn); the boundary must not attempt compensating rollback, because a rollback is another mutation with its own failure mode, and the resolution path is idempotent retry under the gate. **Inventory:** three ndjson write sites (rewrite, append, and a migration backfill whose error is discarded) and four snapshot write sites, one of which is `Service.SaveChatData` in a different package writing `history.json` non-atomically from the in-memory inference context - not another route to the same writer but a different writer with a different content source. **Path construction has three conventions and no constructor:** `<rootDir>/chats/<id>` at nine sites, `<projectRoot>/Chats/<id>` at seven in the memory package, and `<projectRoot>/Chats/<name>` in chat/service - the last addressing a conversation by display name while persistence uses ID, so on a case-insensitive volume the two can reach one directory under two identities, beside the known creation-collision defect. **Verdict, stated plainly:** confinement to one package is false in three independent ways, so the negative assertion is a heuristic today - it would catch a new literal write and would not catch a writer that assembles the name, and it would not establish completeness. Structural form needs three unauthorised changes: one directory constructor with basenames as constants, a `ConversationPath` type constructible only by it so a runtime-built string cannot enter the API, and removal or routing of the second history writer. Only then do the two checkable facts - no literal basename outside the boundary, and no raw string accepted where a path type is required - amount to proof within scope, and even then the non-history files stay outside the claim. | `4d92ebc0b3e49394d13ef7dedb4bef369ff28c79391b8802a657a8e752befe74` | 8650 | 121 |

| 566 | [TASK-03-OQ7D-NONHISTORY-OWNERSHIP-2026-11-22.md](<architecture/TASK-03-OQ7D-NONHISTORY-OWNERSHIP-2026-11-22.md>) | Architecture | TASK-03 OQ-7d: non-history ownership and lifecycle inventory (`TASK-03-OQ7D-NONHISTORY-OWNERSHIP-2026-11-22`) | CURRENT - **Read-only inventory; 19 clauses; V3.1 unchanged; no implementation authorized.** Begins by correcting three of this branch's own published claims. **Withdrawn:** the case-collision claim of row 565 - `chats` and `Chats` cannot collapse because the roots differ, `%APPDATA%/HOWLChat` for persistence versus the installation root for chat.Service and the memory package, so these are two separate trees rather than one directory under two identities; the identity difference (ID versus display name) stands, the collision consequence does not. **Corrected:** `memories.json` is not one contested file with two uncoordinated writers - the two writers address different files in different trees with different schemas, so the defect is two divergent memories stores rather than a race. **Corrected:** the second history writer is dormant, reachable only through the `NativeTestSaveChatData` seam, not a live production path - the finding stands but its severity is materially lower. **Inventory of twelve non-history files** across four writer packages with a reachability column: meta, world, scenario, characters, lorebooks and memories written by SaveChat; memories_backup, candidates and auto_capture in the install tree; continuity_config whose writer has no caller at all; chat.json and memories_snapshot reachable only through the test seam. None uses DurableStore and three write non-atomically. **Ownership:** identity, projections, **user-authored** (memories, the only irreplaceable non-history data) and application-owned settings and caches - seven of twelve are application-owned, so directory adjacency establishes nothing. **Lifecycle:** duplication copies seven files and drops five, wipe stages only the app-data tree, deletion removes only the app-data directory, so every install-tree file survives both. **Outside-scope finding:** a shadow conversation tree at `ProjectRoot()/Chats/<id|name>` which V3.1 never scans, so a `history.json` there would be reported Missing while present elsewhere - recorded as OQ-7d-i, deliberately not absorbed. Partial success restated as a three-valued result whose middle case must never render as a no-op, with retry semantics still unproven (OQ-7d-ii). | `0cbcaff8d41110306989bf663a8baac9a92442614d9b3c95974262470662c1f0` | 9609 | 115 |

| 567 | [TASK-03-OQ7B-LOCK-SCOPE-2026-11-22.md](<architecture/TASK-03-OQ7B-LOCK-SCOPE-2026-11-22.md>) | Architecture | TASK-03 OQ-7b: lock scope, read-only investigation (`TASK-03-OQ7B-LOCK-SCOPE-2026-11-22`) | CURRENT - **Read-only investigation; 20 clauses, 8 tests; V3.1 unchanged; no implementation authorized.** Two guardrails carried forward: reachability is four-valued (production, test-seam, dormant, unestablished) so a claim about the production build cannot drift into a claim about every build configuration; and "memories.json is the only irreplaceable non-history data" is an inventory conclusion, not a guarantee, to be established per file before any wipe, duplication or deletion work. **What the lock covers today:** `LockPath()` is the document path plus `.lock`, held only during that document's write - so the marker is serialised and the log, snapshot, companions and the active-chat pointer are guarded by nothing, and `SaveChat` couples the pointer write to the transition on the hot path. **Four scopes compared:** per-document is insufficient by construction; per-conversation is the minimum sufficient scope because all four artefacts live in one directory and contention stays O(1); per-root is correct but serialises every conversation, turning a 105 ms rewrite into a global stall and a slow volume into repeated Busy; global across both trees is unnecessary because no operation spans them and would overstate the claim. **The constraint that decides it:** a lock file inside the conversation directory breaks creation (no directory yet), deletion (an open handle blocks RemoveAll on Windows) and wipe (an open handle blocks a directory rename), so the lock belongs in a sibling namespace outside `chats/` - placement, not preference - and creation then has a place to lock before MkdirAll, which is what rows 550's second-resolution collision needs. **Deadlock:** wipe needs a root-scoped lock, so the active-chat pointer must leave the conversation transition and be acquired only after the conversation lock is released, leaving one upward edge held by wipe alone. **Refusal, not timeout, is the specified observable;** and the test seam writes the installation tree, so per-conversation locking never covers it and the claim must exclude it by configuration. Five conditions under which per-conversation scope is sufficient. | `f46e81e7aac2c4c2f28df5e73f99f5bdb28739cddc45f3e91a65944ee56143b3` | 9457 | 120 |

| 568 | [TASK-03-OQ7C-REENTRANCY-2026-11-22.md](<architecture/TASK-03-OQ7C-REENTRANCY-2026-11-22.md>) | Architecture | TASK-03 OQ-7c: re-entrancy, read-only call-graph investigation (`TASK-03-OQ7C-REENTRANCY-2026-11-22`) | CURRENT - **Read-only investigation; 18 clauses, 8 tests; V3.1 unchanged; no implementation authorized.** Begins by deciding the two qualifications from OQ-7b. **Seam exclusion is a build fact:** both native test seam files carry `//go:build native_test_seam` with an `_off.go` counterpart, so the seam is absent from a default build - but `Service.SaveChatData` itself is untagged and therefore remains compiled and uncalled, so the correct statement is "the seam is build-excluded, the writer is present but unreachable", and a static assertion must still list it. **Root derivation is not yet stable or authoritative:** `getAppDataDir` uses the user home directory and `paths.IsolationActive()` switches roots entirely, so different profiles, redirection or isolation resolve different locks for what a user would call one conversation (OQ-7c-i). And removing the deadlock edge does not make the unguarded last-writer-wins `config.json` pointer consistent (OQ-7c-ii). **Call graph:** history mutations do call history mutations - `performFinalSave` runs `performSave` and then `RewriteHistory`, `RenameChat` and `SaveChatAs` both route through `LoadChat`/`NewChat`, and `App.LoadChat` mutates the conversation being left before reading the next. Three paths break the one-lock-one-operation assumption: final-save (two complete history writes in one operation), save-as (two conversations), and load (upward save-then-read). **New finding:** every clean shutdown passes through log subset-of snapshot, because `SaveChat` writes the snapshot first and `RewriteHistory` then rewrites both - transient in the normal case, persistent after a crash in that window. **Callbacks:** no persistence call emits an event inside itself, so the boundary can stay callback-free without changing product behaviour; the two re-entry channels (asynchronous frontend dispatch and the synchronous `devEventSink`) are excluded by rule, and a non-reentrant lock turns re-entry into a bounded `Busy` refusal rather than corruption. Answer: one non-reentrant per-conversation hold suffices with three decompositions and one merged final-save. | `e2961377d0683f086aab1458909dc06efca6a092bfd8bfce7bb008c0b44a84e1` | 8766 | 106 |

| 569 | [TASK-03-OQ7CI-ROOT-AUTHORITY-2026-11-22.md](<architecture/TASK-03-OQ7CI-ROOT-AUTHORITY-2026-11-22.md>) | Architecture | TASK-03 OQ-7c-i: root authority, read-only investigation (`TASK-03-OQ7CI-ROOT-AUTHORITY-2026-11-22`) | CURRENT - **Read-only investigation; 14 clauses, 6 tests; V3.1 unchanged; no implementation authorized.** Establishes the three root derivations and finds one of them unstable in a way nobody had recorded: `ProjectRoot()` searches upward from the executable for `wails.json` or `go.mod` within six levels and otherwise falls back to the current working directory, so a shipped build can adopt an enclosing repository as its data root, and the same binary launched from two directories resolves two different installation roots - splitting every non-history file while history stays put. `getAppDataDir()` is recomputed per persistence manager and isolation resolves once via `sync.Once` with a pre-startup validation that exits 2 on an unusable root; no configuration can relocate a root at runtime, since `AppConfig` holds only `LastChatID`. **Answer:** the authoritative conversation root is the history root, identical across production instances of one user on one machine and independent of launch location; different users, isolated runs and roaming-redirected profiles are all deliberately or necessarily different, and the roaming case is the one that matters because the tree lives under `AppData\Roaming` - two machines can hold the same logical conversation with no shared filesystem and therefore no shared lock. **Two rules:** no lock may be derived from `ProjectRoot()` (its launch dependence would give one conversation two lock identities), and the enforcement claim is machine-scoped per user - "two instances" unqualified is false. Isolation remains the correct case for a different root, being validated outside the project tree with a marker file. | `65d97ac26a6daea30f476cabb9d97132bfc62237787af38045c084280e8c7e67` | 6995 | 93 |
| 570 | [TASK-03-DEFECT-FINALSAVE-WINDOW-2026-11-22.md](<architecture/TASK-03-DEFECT-FINALSAVE-WINDOW-2026-11-22.md>) | Architecture | TASK-03 defect record: the final-save representation window (`TASK-03-DEFECT-FINALSAVE-WINDOW-2026-11-22`) | CURRENT - **Defect record only; not repaired; no implementation authorized.** Records, as its own defect separate from re-entrancy and lock scope, that `performFinalSave` runs `SaveChat` - which writes `history.json` from app state - and then `RewriteHistory`, which rewrites `history.ndjson` and `history.json` again. Every clean shutdown therefore passes through a state where the snapshot is newer than the log, which is `log subset-of snapshot`, row 6, `inconsistent`. Normally the window closes within milliseconds; it persists only if the process dies between the two writes, and on the next launch the loader prefers the non-empty log, so the conversation silently reverts to the older log while the newer snapshot sits beside it. It is a defect rather than a tolerated transient because it occurs on every shutdown, it is the designed order of two writes rather than a race, and it needs no concurrent writer to occur. **Ownership:** writer ordering and crash consistency. Reconciliation keeps reporting `Unavailable / inconsistent` and must not prefer the newer representation, prefer the log because the loader does, or reconstruct the intended history - absorbing it would repeat the mistake row 558 found. A per-conversation lock does not close the window, it only prevents other writers from interleaving while it is open; the repair direction is one write of both representations in one operation. Distinct from OQ-3c-b, which concerns a clear followed by a crash. Reachability is production, found by reading the code, not reproduced in this session. | `79fb9497796d1a1ac437c3abb78143bba546ec3b79786a4b56aa1c9e5f84ce11` | 3498 | 53 |

| 571 | [TASK-03-OQ7CII-POINTER-CONSISTENCY-2026-11-22.md](<architecture/TASK-03-OQ7CII-POINTER-CONSISTENCY-2026-11-22.md>) | Architecture | TASK-03 OQ-7c-ii: active-chat pointer consistency, read-only investigation (`TASK-03-OQ7CII-POINTER-CONSISTENCY-2026-11-22`) | CURRENT - **Read-only investigation; 22 clauses, 9 tests; V3.1 unchanged; no implementation authorized.** `LastChatID` is a mutation target selector, not a representation: ten consumers resolve a content write target from it, including the shutdown rewrite, and no conversation content is derived from it - so its loss cannot lose content, its corruption cannot fabricate content, and it is outside the six-state reachability domain entirely, in both directions. **The structural finding:** `App` holds no active-conversation identifier, so the process knows which conversation it is holding only as `pm.config.LastChatID` - a field mirrored to a file every other process on the profile shares, i.e. process-local state carried across a process boundary. Five write sites, four of them side effects; `SaveChat` repoints on every save, so one field means both "what the user selected" and "what this process last persisted", and last-writer-wins without CAS lets an older instance revert selection (preference only, no content). `saveConfig` is the only non-atomic document write in the package - plain `WriteFile` where every other file gets temp-and-rename - so the field that coordinates selection has the weakest durability in the store. **Recovery rewrites it:** an unreadable `meta.json` makes startup fall back to the newest conversation and repoint the file, an automatic pointer repair that reconciliation must neither imitate nor treat as evidence. **Verdict: selection hint, not authority** - one explicit writer, atomic replace, no CAS, no lock, no generation, loss tolerated, because the worst outcome is a re-selection; conditional on two unauthorized dependencies (an `App`-held active ID, and `SaveChat` no longer writing the pointer). Explicitly answers the opening question: removing the pointer from the conversation lock removes an ordering edge, it does not make two instances agree, and it does not make the pointer-selected shutdown overwrite safe - that remains writer authority. Wipe staging is recorded as the pointer's only window of naming a conversation whose data still exists, at a sibling path outside the root, which the scan must report as unreconciled rather than Missing. | `c014f73f829ea3a07af9ec1c450a523ed91889d12f318a168ea0774d1ea6777a` | 15946 | 160 |

| 572 | [TASK-03-OQ7CIII-REENTRANCY-2026-11-22.md](<architecture/TASK-03-OQ7CIII-REENTRANCY-2026-11-22.md>) | Architecture | TASK-03 OQ-7c-iii: whether anything requires true re-entrancy, read-only investigation (`TASK-03-OQ7CIII-REENTRANCY-2026-11-22`) | CURRENT - **Read-only investigation; 20 clauses, 9 tests; V3.1 unchanged; no implementation authorized.** Keeps five routinely conflated situations apart and finds only one is nominally re-entrancy. **Synchronous nesting exists** at two composition sites (`NewChat` to `SaveChat`; `SaveChatAs` to `LoadChat`, `NewChat`, `SaveChat` and a second `SaveChat`) and collapses by construction under OQ-7's containment shape, where one operation is one critical section over an unexported writer - re-entrancy is a symptom of leaving multi-write operations separately lockable, not a requirement. **Async overlap is the real problem and is not recursion:** two unjoined autosave goroutines exist, neither awaited at shutdown, so a final save can race an autosave of the same conversation; and `performSave` reads the pointer at execution time, so an autosave captured in X and executed after selection moved to Y loads Y and overwrites it with X's in-memory content - the OQ-7c-ii identity defect observed in shipped code. **New contract requirement (O7-157):** every mutation uses the identity bound to the content it mutates, and a deferred operation that finds its conversation no longer bound refuses rather than retargeting - no lock prevents retargeting, since the second writer acquires the wrong conversation's lock legitimately. **Callbacks:** none reach a write; `devEventSink` is a display sink only, prohibited by shape. **Cross-conversation:** at most two simultaneous acquisitions in any authorized content operation, under the deterministic total order; wipe is root-scoped and must not loop per-conversation locks. **Verdict: no product requirement needs re-entrancy** - the contract prohibits it and specifies bounded refusal with four outcomes, of which `refused-reentrant` must be diagnosed above the OS lock so an internal composition bug is never reported as contention. A refused write is not written and must never become a content verdict. Records as an observation, not a repair, that `SaveChatAs` writes the destination twice with an empty intermediate state. | `f1aeed9dcc5fadb0d55c40a5daf784272f306767568693fd301de27444735193` | 12928 | 152 |

| 573 | [TASK-03-OQ7DI-SHADOW-TREE-SCOPE-2026-11-22.md](<architecture/TASK-03-OQ7DI-SHADOW-TREE-SCOPE-2026-11-22.md>) | Architecture | TASK-03 OQ-7d-i: scope of the installation-tree store, read-only investigation (`TASK-03-OQ7DI-SHADOW-TREE-SCOPE-2026-11-22`) | CURRENT - **Read-only investigation; 24 clauses, 9 tests; V3.1 unchanged; no implementation authorized.** **Answer: a mixture, and the mixture is the finding.** `StateRoot()/Chats` is a shared directory namespace, not a store: live user-authored memories and `auto_capture_config` (read and written through App bindings), a settings file with a live reader and no writer, test-seam-only `history.json`/`chat.json`/`memories_snapshot.json`, and a cache with an unestablished production caller. No conversation is ever opened from it - no writer there produces a `meta.json` - so it is neither an independent legacy store nor a migration target; it is active product data with a separate lifecycle, wrapped around test artifacts. **Two corrections to row 566:** the base is `StateRoot()`, not `ProjectRoot()` (equal in production, different under isolation), and `chat.json` has **no reader** - `ExportContentItem` refuses `library.Chats` before `contentItemFile` is reached. **Two live lifecycle findings:** `WipeAllData` removes only `<RootDir>/chats`, so user-authored memories survive a "wipe all data", and the wipe's `SetChatID("")` is a no-op because empty is ignored, so the next memory write recreates the last conversation's shadow directory; deletion likewise leaves the namespace untouched, so orphans accumulate by deletion, wipe and launch from another directory. **Boundary verdict: unchanged** - reconciliation scans `<rootDir>/chats` only, candidacy is decided by `meta.json` (which is what makes the exclusion structural rather than a path rule), shadow directories and orphans get no verdict at all, and a `Healthy` verdict must state that memories were not assessed. Also records that `paths.Roots()` is a target not an authority: its app-data root is `%AppData%\Howl` while persistence writes `%AppData%\Roaming\HOWLChat`, and it labels the installation root non-writable while the memory package writes beneath it. | `20bdd6ec0b08cf0aba052153a8feb1eadc559c8cd5648db159d1da24361ea940` | 11981 | 120 |

| 574 | [TASK-03-OQ7DII-RETRY-IDENTITY-2026-11-22.md](<architecture/TASK-03-OQ7DII-RETRY-IDENTITY-2026-11-22.md>) | Architecture | TASK-03 OQ-7d-ii: retry identity, read-only investigation (`TASK-03-OQ7DII-RETRY-IDENTITY-2026-11-22`) | CURRENT - **Read-only investigation; 48 clauses, 9 tests; V3.1 unchanged; no implementation authorized.** **Verdict: retry identity is not derivable from the existing data. The store can prove non-application; it cannot prove application.** No operation identity exists anywhere in the conversation path - `ChatMeta`, `ChatHistory` and `AppConfig` carry no operation field, `AppendHistoryEntry` takes no key, and the package holds no journal, receipt or counter. **Message identity is positional** at three sites (`app-prompt.go:303`, `app-chat.go:405`, `app-prompt.go:357`), so it cannot key an idempotency check in either direction: it is not unique across time and shifts under a rewrite. `AppendHistoryEntry` (`persistence.go:668-706`) applies unconditionally - no marker read, no generation compare, no content inspection. **A torn append is worse than a duplicate:** the writer discards `n` and never fsyncs (`:702`), the reader stops at the first unmarshalable line and returns the prefix (`:609`), and because the torn log is non-empty the `history.json` fallback never engages (`:589-591`) - so the retry is appended after the tear, becomes part of the invalid line, and every later successful append is invisible too. `RewriteHistory` (`:630-661`) is the only retry-safe writer and only by substitution, never by recognition. **The sole existing idempotency attempt is `recordUserTurn` (`app-chat.go:360-370`)**: in-memory, adjacent-only, exact-content, field-blind, and sound only under an unstated caller protocol - evidence of intent, unusable as precedent. **Binding is absent:** the target is read at execution time (`app-chat.go:314-316`, `app-prompt.go:296-298`), so a retry re-resolves and can retarget; `O7-157` already forbids that but nothing supplies the identity, and the pointer cannot be promoted to it (row 571). **Settlement is not a retry ledger:** `(state, generation, settled_generation)` has no operation identity and cannot answer "did this operation apply"; extending it would give one document two authorities, recreating what row 573 removed. **Five outcomes enumerated; only refusal before mutation is decidable.** Two honest options remain - restrict retry to the refusal class, or require a durable operation identity with seven derived properties (bound at capture, recorded before content, compared in one critical section, terminal settlement, never content-derived, per-conversation, re-execution-refusing) - and retry of an ambiguous append is **prohibited**, since it duplicates or extends the tear. **Precedent and its limit:** `contentimport` already implements the shape (`already_imported` / `operation_active` / `plan_mismatch` / `StateAmbiguous` / `Resume`-only, `:299-313`) but is whole-item and hash-based, so the pattern is idiomatic here and not reusable; `durable.Outcome` (`NotSaved(rename_denied_by_open_reader)`) supplies the failure classification the chat writers lack. **Unsaved work:** three domains must be reported separately (stored/captured/displayed); `_ = a.AppendChatMessage(...)` (`app-prompt.go:138`) already leaves a failed message in memory and out of the authoritative log, restored only by the final rewrite of row 570; a refusal must return `not_attempted`/`not_applied`/`unknown` and may never discard captured content. Reconciliation retries nothing and no retry rule may move a state toward `Healthy`. | `c0033256f2bf1262de075e7314538e6654f99c8e58fa8e4814fbcc2c84aa1bcc` | 25234 | 201 |
| 575 | [TASK-03-OQ7DIII-CALLER-OUTCOMES-2026-11-22.md](<architecture/TASK-03-OQ7DIII-CALLER-OUTCOMES-2026-11-22.md>) | Architecture | TASK-03 OQ-7d-iii: caller-visible outcomes and unsaved-work disposition, read-only investigation (`TASK-03-OQ7DIII-CALLER-OUTCOMES-2026-11-22`) | CURRENT - **Read-only investigation and proposal; 38 clauses, 10 tests; V3.1 unedited; nothing adopted, nothing authorized.** Defines the caller-visible result as seven fields over three independent domains (stored / captured / displayed), not a boolean: `Outcome` (`applied`, `no_op`, `not_attempted`, `not_applied`, `unknown`), `ConversationID`, the three domain statements, `Retry` and `Reason`. `unknown` may never be rendered, logged or summarised as saved; `applied` means observed write success, never durability, because `AppendHistoryEntry` discards the written count and never syncs. **New finding: a sixth position row 574 did not name** - `appendChatMessage` appends to memory (`app-prompt.go:294`), then returns `nil` when no conversation is selected (`:299-301`), so a message can be captured and displayed while nothing is written and the call reports success; blank content is a second, different `nil` (`:282-284`). Only refusal is retryable (`not_attempted` / `not_applied` with the identity still bound); `unknown` is prohibited, and its only honest affordances are retention, explicit discard reported as data loss, or export. **Switch is a disposition point:** `LoadChat` attempts a save, prints a warning and continues (`app-chat.go:96-99`), then `updateAppStateFromChat` replaces the in-memory history (`:106`), so the outgoing conversation's captured turn is unrecoverable in-process - a later rewrite may persist captured work only before the switch, never after, which narrows row 574's "restored by the final rewrite" wording. **Shutdown** makes one attempt via `runStage` (`app-lifecycle.go:121`) and reports failure only to stdout; a failed save and a clean one are indistinguishable, and success does not make the turn authoritative until both row 570 writes complete. **No conforming caller exists:** the append result is discarded at `app-prompt.go:138` and at three frontend sites (`chat-input.js:247`, `chat-actions.js:77,172`) via `.catch(() => {})`, with four `_ = a.performSave()` sites and console-only autosave failures; the required rule is a negative assertion over discard sites, not a checklist. **Binding is separated from operation identity:** conversation binding is required for refusal-only retry; durable operation identity is required only for ambiguous retry, so the safe policy needs no journal. Recommends refusal-only retry as the baseline, unadopted; the owner selects. | `64629096dcbdda3240a00d5f729a464597235d3599dbfad63dc2750e3e4ec7c8` | 21825 | 199 |
| 576 | [TASK-03-OPEN-QUESTION-REGISTER-AUDIT-2026-11-22.md](<architecture/TASK-03-OPEN-QUESTION-REGISTER-AUDIT-2026-11-22.md>) | Architecture | TASK-03 open-question register audit and consolidation point (`TASK-03-OPEN-QUESTION-REGISTER-AUDIT-2026-11-22`) | CURRENT - **Audit and proposal; 32 clauses, 6 checks; V3.1 unedited; no question closed here; nothing authorized.** Opens by recording that there is no open-question register - the state of the investigation exists only as trailing tables in ten documents, which violates the pack's own no-hidden-conditions principle. | 577 | [TASK-03-OQ1-DISCONNECTED-ROOT-2026-11-22.md](<architecture/TASK-03-OQ1-DISCONNECTED-ROOT-2026-11-22.md>) | Architecture | TASK-03 OQ-1: disconnected-root classification, read-only investigation (`TASK-03-OQ1-DISCONNECTED-ROOT-2026-11-22`) | CURRENT - **Read-only investigation; 36 clauses, 9 tests; outcome Amend; V3.1 unedited; nothing adopted, nothing authorized.** First of row 576's six baseline questions, resolved under Amend/Clarify/Retain. **Outcome: Amend, on observed evidence.** A disposable probe on `go1.27.3 windows/amd64` found that an unresolvable UNC root returns errno 53 (`ERROR_BAD_NETPATH`) and `os.IsNotExist` / `errors.Is(fs.ErrNotExist)` are **true** at both the root and the conversation path, while a reachable host with a missing share returns errno 67 and both predicates are **false** - so the same unreachable root class yields opposite verdicts from the obvious Go idiom, and one of them is `Missing`. Local absence (errno 2/3) is correctly `ErrNotExist`, which is why local tests do not reveal the inversion. **The defect:** `V3-39` row 1 reads "conversation directory absent" with no root precondition, while row 2's "I/O error" overlaps it; first-match resolves the overlap toward the unsafe row. On 53 the scanner asserts deletion from a location it never observed, which is the exact inversion of "a scan error must never become Missing" - `V3-47` forbids the transition but not the one-step arrival. **The amendment:** new terms `root_state` (`observed` / `absent` / `unobservable`) and `root_class` (`local-fixed` / `non-local` / `unknown`, from UNC shape and `GetDriveTypeW` 3 versus every other value, treated fail-closed); row 1 gains a three-part precondition (root observed, class local-fixed, child errno 2/3); on non-local or unknown roots `Missing` is **unreachable** and an absence-shaped child is `Unavailable / absence_unverified`, a new reason; transport and access codes (53, 67 and the source-derived 21/55/59/64/121/1231/1232) are Unavailable on every class; absence may never be derived from a sentinel alone. Double observation is considered and rejected - it narrows but cannot close the disconnect window and buys no proof. **Reachability:** production only conditionally, through a redirected `AppData\Roaming` (source-derived, not reproduced), or an isolation root (operator setup); a root unreachable at startup fails `MkdirAll` and no scan runs, so the realistic case is a mid-session disconnect. **Correction carried:** `OQR-13`/`OQR-14`/`OQR-26` said no prerequisite can change a row; the precise statement is that a prerequisite cannot change a row's rule but can change capability availability and row reachability - rows 16-17 are unreachable, not redefined. Rows 575-576 are otherwise unchanged; the retry-only boundary and the `applied`/`stored`/`durable` separation are carried, not reopened. | `831641e401b231a8d23a93e784919a1b641fb22cfb0c0425d128937dd21f61ba` | 19903 | 168 |
| 578 | [TASK-03-OQ2-ALIAS-IDENTITY-2026-11-22.md](<architecture/TASK-03-OQ2-ALIAS-IDENTITY-2026-11-22.md>) | Architecture | TASK-03 OQ-2: alias paths and conversation identity, read-only investigation (`TASK-03-OQ2-ALIAS-IDENTITY-2026-11-22`) | CURRENT - **Read-only investigation; 36 clauses, 9 tests; outcome Clarify; V3.1 unedited; nothing adopted, nothing authorized.** **Answer: aliases add neither a location nor a conversation.** Two paths that resolve to one physical directory are one candidate and one logical conversation; a copy is a second location claiming the same logical ID and is a conflict to report, never to merge. **Observed** with a disposable junction fixture: junction, case, trailing-dot, 8.3 and `\\?\` spellings all return the same `(volume serial, file index)` while every path-string comparison is false; a copy has a different index; `EvalSymlinks` does **not** resolve the junction while `GetFinalPathNameByHandle` does; identity survives rename, and the junction then dangles with `IsNotExist` because it stores a path, not a file ID. **PathService supplies containment, not identity:** `FileIdentity` is exported and `identityOf`/`resolvePhysical` are correct, but `identityOf` has no caller and no exported function returns or compares an identity; `CheckContained(base, alias)` returns nil through the junction, proving the physical containment path works while the identity API is absent. **Four identities separated:** locator (path; never a key), physical (volume+index; dedup and lock key; changes on copy/restore), logical (`meta.ID`, else directory name; verdict attachment, ownership, retry binding; survives rename/restore) and operation (row 574). **A live conflation:** `GetChatList` enumerates by directory name but returns `ChatSummary.ID = meta.ID` (`persistence.go:502,522`) while `LoadChat` treats its argument as the directory name (`:260`), so a renamed directory or a manual copy is listed under an ID that cannot be loaded; `RenameChat` changes only `Name` and nothing enforces name/ID agreement. **Rules:** one candidate per physical directory; dedup by identity only when `Volume != 0 && Index != 0` and collision-free; verdict attaches to logical identity; two physical dirs with one logical ID are reported, never merged (row 550 owns the shape); a dangling alias is not a conversation and must not make the target `Missing` (row 577); lock key is physical identity, verified before and after acquisition, refusing when uncomputable rather than falling back to a path; `SaveChatAs` already regenerates the logical ID via `NewChat`, a manual copy does not. Network-provider identity (mapped drive vs UNC, SMB stability) is **Unestablished**; the nonzero guard fails closed. No `V3-39` row or reason changes - the amendment is a term set and a candidate rule, not a new state. | `341052761db4df273eb6180e9505f57801976ae0c0f8b440490d8e1f68144d0f` | 18245 | 168 |
| 579 | [TASK-03-OQ3BB-TERMINATOR-2026-11-22.md](<architecture/TASK-03-OQ3BB-TERMINATOR-2026-11-22.md>) | Architecture | TASK-03 OQ-3b-b: the missing final newline, read-only investigation (`TASK-03-OQ3BB-TERMINATOR-2026-11-22`) | CURRENT - **Read-only investigation; 38 clauses, 8 tests; outcome Amend; V3.1 unedited; nothing adopted, nothing authorized.** **Answer: the final newline is a writer invariant, not a format requirement.** All three production writers emit it (`AppendHistoryEntry` at `persistence.go:688-702`, `RewriteHistory` at `:641-648`, the first-append migration at `:678-686`), but the loader is a `bufio.Scanner` (`:598-616`) that returns the final token at EOF, so an unterminated complete record loads. **Observed with the real `LoadChat`:** three records terminated and three with the last unterminated both load **3**; a partial third loads 2; a lone `"\n"` loads 0; two CRLF records load 2; snapshot-only loads 2; an unterminated two-record log beside a three-record snapshot loads 2 (log wins). **A parseable final line is a complete record** - a JSON object ends at its closing brace, so no fields are missing - and a write cut exactly at the boundary is byte-identical to a foreign unterminated record. **The real harm is the next append:** onto an unterminated tail it concatenates two objects into one line, and the probe shows the loader then returns **1** entry instead of 3. **Strict rejection is both false and ineffective:** `V3-08` would call a file whose records all load `malformed` / row 7 Damaged when no snapshot holds content, while the read-only scanner (`V3-55`) cannot stop the append that causes the loss. **The bounded amendment:** `V3-08` becomes "every non-blank line parses; the final record may be unterminated"; `tail` is recorded as a diagnostic, not a state; row 7 `truncated` narrows to an unparseable final line; rows 14-15 carry reason `unterminated_tail` while remaining Healthy; `V3-09` (lone newline malformed) is explicitly retained; and the gate may not append onto an unterminated tail - it terminates the tail in the same critical section or refuses, never concatenates. Rewrites repair the tail; row 6 prefix relations and the six states are unchanged. **The two clauses are one change:** accepting the classification without the writer rule would turn a false Damaged into silent record loss. Repair ownership stays OQ-4; legacy prevalence and torn-at-boundary frequency remain Unestablished; no storage redesign is decided or excluded. | `17737609e32f1ae92a8defbaaeef8c60cec86196664da960522c634c9076e5b1` | 15386 | 168 |
| 580 | [TASK-03-ROW550-CREATION-COLLISION-2026-11-22.md](<architecture/TASK-03-ROW550-CREATION-COLLISION-2026-11-22.md>) | Architecture | TASK-03 row 550: creation collision, read-only investigation (`TASK-03-ROW550-CREATION-COLLISION-2026-11-22`) | CURRENT - **Read-only investigation; 38 clauses, 8 tests; outcome Clarify plus a creation-side prerequisite; V3.1 unedited; no repair authorized.** **All four damaging outcomes are reachable:** creation can adopt an existing directory, overwrite metadata and companions, leave the old log in place and hand the caller a new conversation that is really the old one. **Observed cross-process:** two child processes released in a forced same second both targeted `2026-11-22T17-15-55Z`; one failed on a sharing violation on the fixed `meta.json.tmp` path, not on collision detection, and the other succeeded, leaving one directory with meta B and log from-B. **Observed same-process:** a same-second `SaveChatAs` returned source_id == copy_id, renamed the source metadata to "duplicate" and replaced its snapshot; the caller believes a copy exists. **Observed overwrite of a pre-existing conversation:** a conversation seeded at the predicted second with `memories.json = [{"text":"precious"}]` was silently adopted by `NewChat("intruder")` - same ID, nil error, meta renamed, **memories.json replaced by `[]`**, history.json `[]`, while history.ndjson kept the old record and `LoadChat` returned name=intruder with texts=[old]. Chat-scoped memories are the irreplaceable user-authored data (rows 566/573), so this is direct data loss. **Paths:** `NewChat` (`MkdirAll`, no existence check), `SaveChatAs` (via `NewChat`), and implicit creation in `SaveChat` and `RewriteHistory`, both of which `MkdirAll` an arbitrary ID; `AppendHistoryEntry` does not create. **Caller cannot tell:** `NewChat` returns in-memory `ChatData`, never reads back or compares an existing meta; `App.NewChat` then clears the in-memory history and points the memory service at the adopted ID. **Sufficiency:** unique non-clock ID + exclusive claim (`Mkdir`, not `MkdirAll`) + no implicit creation in other writers + safe commit ordering (meta written last or temp-dir move) are sufficient for "never silently adopt, merge, overwrite or redirect"; a full transaction is not required for that end result, though it adds crash atomicity. A creation lock is not the primary guard; `LastChatID` is never ownership. **Reconciliation: Clarify, not Damaged.** The residue can be a valid, readable conversation (the overwrite case loads cleanly), so rows 10/12/14-15 decide by evidence; V3.2 gains a limitation that Healthy/Pending assert content availability, not exclusive creation, ID uniqueness, companion ownership or uncompromised ownership, and adds no state, reason or repair. **Correction:** `RC5-7`'s "report a collision as an anomaly" is not implementable as stated because the surviving shape is coherent; `RC5-8`-`RC5-10` stand. `V3-73`'s row-550 item closes with no row change; the creation repair remains a separately owned, unauthorized task. | `ec3cb718cee3e5d15be2c08df1e0eb43e2b40c7af2bc7f51510363f661a48752` | 16775 | 171 |

content belongs to the intended logical conversation (no); creation claimed a previously unused identity (no); user-authored companion data preserved (no). **`Healthy` restated:** observed content present, valid and mutually consistent within the permitted prefix relation, attributable to the marker's generation, or legitimately empty/closed under its marker;
