| 582 | [TASK-03-MEMORIES-LIMITATION-2026-11-22.md](<architecture/TASK-03-MEMORIES-LIMITATION-2026-11-22.md>) | Architecture | TASK-03 memories limitation, read-only investigation (`TASK-03-MEMORIES-LIMITATION-2026-11-22`) | CURRENT - **Read-only investigation; 39 clauses, 8 tests; outcome Clarify; V3.1 unedited; no memory state, lifecycle, repair or implementation authorized.** **Answer:** two different memory stores, and the conversation verdict belongs to neither. **Conversation health must never imply memory health.** **Two files share the name `memories.json`:** the conversation companion at `<pm.RootDir()>/chats/<id>/memories.json` (`[]interface{}`, written by `NewChat`/`SaveChat`/`SaveChatAs`, dormant because the production save payload has no `memories` key) and the live user-authored records at `StateRoot()/Chats/<id>/memories.json` (`[]MemoryRecord`, written by Add/Edit/Delete/import, **outlives the conversation**). `memories_backup.json` is rewritten as literal `{}` before every save and has **no reader**; it cannot restore anything. `memories_snapshot.json` is read at init and has **no writer found**. `auto_capture_config.json` and `continuity_config.json` are app-owned settings; `candidates.json` is cache; generated lore is global content. **There is no global memory store** - a `global`-scope record still lives in one chat's shadow directory. **Lifecycle:** `DeleteChat` removes the candidate dir and leaves the shadow records, and the memory service keeps the deleted ID, so the next write recreates its directory; `WipeAllData` removes the conversations and leaves every memory, and `SetChatID("")` is a no-op, so the last ID is retained; rename preserves the ID and the association; copying/moving a conversation does not carry the shadow store; `StateRoot()` moves with launch location, making memories unreachable without deleting them; `SaveChatAs` copies only the dormant companion, and a same-second collision makes both conversations share one shadow store. **Absence at one scope is not deletion** - `loadMemories` returns `[]` for a missing file, so zero is not evidence of zero memories. **Scanner:** may observe presence, readability and parseability at a named root; cannot assess completeness, authorship, ownership, correct association, backup validity or recoverability without becoming a memory authority; no write, repair, delete, dedup, restore or precedence choice. Reading the shadow store would extend the boundary, so it belongs to a **separate read-only disclosure that feeds no row**. **Disclosure findings, not states:** `unassessed`, `not_found_at_scope` (never "deleted"/"empty"), `unreadable` (never zero), `observed` (never complete/intact), `orphaned` (memory data without a conversation; never Missing/Damaged, no delete prompt). Every conversation verdict carries `memories: unassessed`; no aggregation into "all data healthy". **Amendment:** scope clause, non-implication clause, separate disclosure clause, `V3-73` closure; no row, state, reason or transition changes. Memory lifecycle/recovery, if needed, is a separate ownership problem and is not obtained by adding states. | `157b92142251322c5ba1e31d2ea37e9dea8d86b9af7554531432565e4296f14a` | 17492 | 158 |

**Scope of these rows.** Registration records identity, metadata and navigation only. Contract substance stays in the papers; status authority stays with the contract register above, which remains the source of truth for the pack. This block defines no lifecycle, deletion or retention policy.

**D2.** The register records the 2026-11-21 D2 wording reconciliation and the `OD-D2-COPY` scope transition: memory transfer is a copy operation, the source remains parent-owned, each destination copy is independently owned, provenance survives deletion of the source, and no deletion cascades into copies. D2's questions remain unresolved — `orphaned` is still undefined for legacy, unknown-origin and interrupted-lifecycle records; chat-scoped records, conversation-event and state-history retention, and privacy deletion remain open; destination ownership of a copy remains unresolved (`FC1-22g`, `FC1-MEMORY-COPY-DESTINATION`); and FC-1's five memory-copy questions (`FC1-MEMORY-COPY-NOTIFY`, `FC1-MEMORY-COPY-DESTINATION`, `FC1-MEMORY-COPY-TIMING`, `FC1-MEMORY-NOTICE-SCOPE`, `FC1-MEMORY-COPY-MERGE`) stay open. Nothing in this section resolves them.

**No implementation authorization.** Registering these eight artifacts authorizes nothing: no code, schema, API, migration, test, model or process change follows from it, and M0A remains separate and unauthorized.

## TASK-03 deletion architecture and S-1 correction, registered 2026-11-22

| # | File | Family | Role | Current relevance / placement | SHA-256 | Bytes | Lines |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 589 | [TASK-03-DELETION-AND-S1-CORRECTION-2026-11-22.md](<architecture/TASK-03-DELETION-AND-S1-CORRECTION-2026-11-22.md>) | Architecture | Deletion architecture decision and S-1 correction pass (`TASK-03-DELETION-AND-S1-CORRECTION-2026-11-22`) | CURRENT - **Decision and implementation record; 10 clauses (`DE-01`-`DE-10`) plus four corrections (`S1C-01`-`S1C-04`); uncommitted, user-visible behaviour not demonstrated.** **Windows deletion:** `SHFileOperationW` is superseded by `IFileOperation`, which reports accurate HRESULTs, but both recycle only **"if possible"** - `FOF_ALLOWUNDO` preserves undo information if possible, and a volume without a bin or with a full one still ends in permanent deletion without error, so **no Windows API guarantees recycling**. `recycleTree` therefore calls `SHFileOperationW` with `FO_DELETE|FOF_ALLOWUNDO|FOF_NOCONFIRMATION|FOF_SILENT|FOF_NOERRORUI` on a COM-initialised OS-locked thread with the double-NUL source the API requires, and **this program holds no permanent-delete path**: a refusal leaves the files and reports leftovers. `IFileOperation` was declined for now - COM vtable interop for one call path, revisitable in one function. **Obsolete path removed, not wrapped:** `removeTree` deleted; the only `os.RemoveAll` left is in `recycle_other.go`, compiled only off Windows. **Tombstone kept and justified:** a blocked delete leaves the directory, so without intent the next scan re-lists it - the Recycle Bin does not change that. `deleted.ndjson` is append-only *intent*, written before removal, owned solely by `PersistenceManager`, never proof of destruction, never compacted in this slice (the index will consume it and owns compaction). **Corrections:** the final-save window closes by replacing save-reload-rewrite with one load and one `CommitChat` (companions, then log, then snapshot, under one hold, so an interruption can only leave the log newer and the loader prefers the log); creation claims its directory with an exclusive `Mkdir` and retries with a suffixed identifier, so the row 580 silent adoption is no longer reachable through `NewChat` or `SaveChatAs`; a refused append is logged and emitted as `chat:unsaved` instead of discarded. **Not changed, with reason:** `SaveChat`/`RewriteHistory`/`AppendHistoryEntry` still `MkdirAll` their directory, so a write can still materialise a non-existent conversation - the creation side of `V3-72a`, which needs its direct-save callers migrated and is its own pass. **Ownership table recorded:** membership has **no authority** yet - it is derived from directory contents, so nothing can report a missing or orphaned conversation;
